Fortifying spiritual leaders, night devotions build unshakeable moral strength required to guide communities through severe trials. Illuminating the grave and Resurrection Day, nighttime worship converts dark earthly hours into eternal celestial light. Encouraging regular night prayer, believers establish a private sanctuary of peace in the late hours of the night.

## Sūrah Ṭā-Hā 20:131

> Do not let your eyes crave what We have allowed some of the disbelievers to enjoy; the ˹fleeting˺ splendour of this worldly life, which We test them with. But your Lord’s provision ˹in the Hereafter˺ is far better and more lasting

**Prohibition Against Coveting Worldly Splendor (Zahrat Al-Ḥayāt Al-Dunyā)**

God issues a profound ethical command to Prophet Muḥammad and all believers about materialistic distraction: *wa-lā tamuddanna ʿaynayka ilā mā mattaʿnā bihi azwājan minhum zahrata al-ḥayāti al-dunyā li-naftinahum fīhi wa-rizqu rabbika khayrun wa-abqā* ('Do not extend your eyes toward what We have given some of them to enjoy of the splendor of worldly life, as a test for them. The provision of your Lord is far better and more lasting'). God commands humanity not to gaze with envy, longing, or covetousness (*lā tamuddanna ʿaynayk*) at the temporary material luxuries granted to disbelievers and worldly elites. Material wealth, political power, and physical ornament are designated as *zahrat al-ḥayāt al-dunyā*—a fragile, short-lived 'flower of worldly life' designed merely as a divine test (*li-naftinahum fīhi*). True, permanent wealth is found in the spiritual provisions granted by Allāh (*rizqu rabbika khayrun wa-abqā*). Prohibiting covetous gazing (*lā tamuddanna ʿaynayk*), God protects human consciousness from envy, consumerist anxiety, and spiritual discontent. Describing worldly luxury as *zahrat al-ḥayāt al-dunyā* ('the flower of worldly life'), the Quran highlights that material wealth is attractive yet fragile, rapidly wilting and passing away. Revealing the purpose of worldly wealth (*li-naftinahum fīhi*), material abundance is not a sign of divine love, but a severe divine test evaluating moral stewardship. Exalting divine spiritual provision (*rizqu rabbika khayrun wa-abqā*), faith, wisdom, Quranic guidance, and eternal Paradise are incomparably superior and permanent. Protecting believers from materialistic traps, verse 131 re-anchors human value systems in spiritual capital rather than financial accumulation. Dismantling feelings of inferiority, believers recognize that worldly elites possessing wealth without faith are undergoing a perilous divine test. Guiding economic ethics, verse 131 promotes contented simple living (*qunūʿah*) and freedom from consumerist competition.

**Worldly Luxury as a Divine Test (Li-Naftinahum Fīh)**

The explicit divine declaration *li-naftinahum fīhi* ('so that We may test them by it') dismantles a major human misconception. Uninformed human beings often assume that granting vast wealth, health, and power to an individual indicates divine favor and approval. The Quran corrects this error, revealing that material abundance is a severe, dangerous divine trial (*fitnah*). Wealth evaluates whether a person will become arrogant, greedy, and rebellious, or remain humble, generous, and obedient. Defining material abundance as a test (*fitnah*), granting wealth evaluates moral stewardship rather than signaling divine pleasure. Exposing the danger of abundance, wealth frequently breeds arrogance, self-sufficiency, and forgetfulness of God (*kufrān al-niʿmah*). Explaining gradual divine enticement (*istidrāj*), granting ungrateful sinners continuous luxury without faith leads them deeper into spiritual ruin (6:44). Equalizing human conditions, poverty tests patience (*ṣabr*), while wealth tests gratitude (*shukr*) and generosity. Relieving envy, recognizing that a neighbor's luxury is their personal test frees the believer from longing for their burden. Promoting responsible stewardship, wealthy believers use their financial resources to establish zakāh, sadaqah, and social justice. Preparing for accounting, every dollar of wealth will be examined on Judgment Day about how it was earned and spent.

**The Superiority of Divine Provision (Rizqu Rabbika Khayrun Wa-Abqā)**

The verse concludes with an unshakeable comparative truth: *wa-rizqu rabbika khayrun wa-abqā* ('and the provision of your Lord is far better and more lasting'). *Rizq* in Quranic terminology encompasses both earthly provisions (lawful sustenance, family, health) and spiritual provisions (faith, Quranic understanding, wisdom, prayer, peace of mind, and eternal Paradise). Divine spiritual provision is *khayr* (superior in quality) and *abqā* (eternal in duration). Defining divine provision (*rizqu rabbik*), authentic *rizq* includes monotheistic faith, moral clarity, inner peace, and Paradise. Defining *khayr* (better), spiritual wealth bestows deep, unshakeable contentment that money can never purchase. Defining *abqā* (more lasting), spiritual assets and Paradise endure eternally, surviving physical death and cosmic dissolution.

## Sūrah Ṭā-Hā 20:132

> Bid your people to pray, and be diligent in ˹observing˺ it. We do not ask you to provide. It is We Who provide for you. And the ultimate outcome is ˹only˺ for ˹the people of˺ righteousness

God addresses Prophet Muḥammad and every head of a household with a vital familial and spiritual mandate: *wa'mur ahlaka bi-l-ṣalāti wa-ṣṭabir ʿalayhā lā nas'aluka rizqan naḥnu narzuquka wa-al-ʿāqibatu li-l-taqwā* ('And bid your family to pray and be steadfast in it. We do not ask you for provision; We provide for you. And the good end is for righteousness'). God reassures believers that prayer does not diminish earning capacity; God guarantees physical provision (*naḥnu narzuquk*), and ultimate victory (*al-ʿāqibah*) belongs exclusively to righteousness (*taqwā*). Establishing ultimate cosmic law (*wa-al-ʿāqibatu li-l-taqwā*), divine success, lasting honor, and salvation belong exclusively to God-consciousness. Integrating work and worship, verse 132 teaches that economic pursuits must never eclipse daily devotional obligations.

**The Guarantee of Divine Sustenance (Naḥshuruhu / Naḥnu Narzuquk)**

A common whisper from Satan is that taking time out of a busy workday for prayer will cause financial loss, career failure, or business decline. Verse 132 completely demolishes this fear. God declares: *lā nas'aluka rizqan naḥnu narzuquk* ('We do not ask you for provision; We provide for you'). God does not require human beings to provide for Him; rather, God is the sole Provider (*al-Razzāq*). Prioritizing prayer brings divine barakah (blessing) into one's time, income, and life. Demolishing financial anxieties (*lā nas'aluka rizqan*), God clarifies that worshipping Him never threatens physical economic survival. Guaranteeing divine provision (*naḥnu narzuquk*), God takes direct responsibility for sustaining those who prioritize His worship. Inculcating divine *barakah* (blessing), prayer increases efficiency, wisdom, and providential blessings in lawful income. Correcting materialistic worldviews, money is merely a tool; true provision (*rizq*) comes directly from the Almighty Sustainer. Relieving psychological stress, entrusting financial security to God frees the heart from suffocating economic dread. Integrating daily schedule around prayer, pausing business for *ṣalāh* honors the True King and invites providential success. Demonstrating historical reality, Muslim communities that prioritized prayer achieved unmatched civilizational, scientific, and economic heights.

**The Eternal Maxim: Wa-Al-ʿĀqibatu Li-L-Taqwā (The Good End is for Righteousness)**

The verse concludes with an unshakeable Quranic maxim: *wa-al-ʿāqibatu li-l-taqwā* ('and the ultimate good end belongs to God-consciousness'). In the short term, worldly oppressors, dishonest businessmen, and corrupt elites may appear to achieve financial superiority and political dominance. However, the ultimate final outcome (*al-ʿāqibah*) in both history and the Hereafter belongs exclusively to those who cultivate God-consciousness (*taqwā*). Establishing an eternal law (*wa-al-ʿāqibatu li-l-taqwā*), ultimate victory, honor, and salvation belong strictly to moral righteousness. RefUTING short-sighted worldly metrics, immediate material gain achieved through dishonesty or neglecting prayer is a temporary delusion. Consoling struggling believers, standing firm upon righteousness guarantees divine vindication and ultimate success. Defining true success (*al-ʿāqibah*), ending life with faith, receiving divine forgiveness, and entering Paradise constitutes real victory. Anchoring family legacy in *taqwā*, raising a righteous, praying family secures ongoing spiritual rewards that outlast physical death. Guiding social and political ethics, lasting civilizational stability requires anchoring governance in moral integrity and God-consciousness. Inspiring daily perseverance, knowing that *taqwā* wins the final outcome empowers believers to remain steadfast during earthly trials.

## Sūrah Ṭā-Hā 20:133

> They demand, “If only he could bring us a sign from his Lord!” Have they not ˹already˺ received a confirmation of what is in earlier Scriptures

God addresses the persistent, disingenuous demands of Meccan polytheists for arbitrary physical miracles: *wa-qālū law-lā ya'tīnā bi-āyatin min rabbihi a-wa-lam ta'tihim bayyinatu mā fī al-ṣuḥufi al-ūlā* ('They say: If only he brought us a sign from his Lord! Has there not come to them clear proof of what was in the earlier scriptures?'). God refutes their excuse, pointing to the ultimate, living miracle already in their hands: the Holy Quran, which serves as a comprehensive, clear proof (*bayyinah*) confirming and fulfilling all truths contained in previous divine scriptures (*al-ṣuḥuf al-ūlā*). RefUTING disingenuous demands for miracles (*law-lā ya'tīnā bi-āyah*), God exposes that polytheists demanded signs out of stubborn defiance rather than a genuine search for truth. Presenting the Holy Quran as the ultimate miracle (*bayyinatu mā fī al-ṣuḥufi al-ūlā*), scripture provides unshakeable rational, linguistic, and historical proof.

**The Quran as the Supreme Confirmation of Earlier Scriptures (Al-Ṣuḥuf Al-Ūlā)**

The Quranic phrase *bayyinatu mā fī al-ṣuḥufi al-ūlā* ('clear proof of what was in the earlier scriptures') highlights the profound historical and theological harmony of divine revelation. The Quran contains pristine accounts of past prophets, ancient nations, moral laws, and eschatological realities that match the uncorrupted core of the Torah (*al-Tawrāt*), Psalms (*al-Zabūr*), and Gospel (*al-Injīl*). An unlettered Prophet in Mecca delivering such precise historical and theological wisdom is absolute proof of divine authorship. Highlighting scriptural harmony, the Quran confirms the core monotheistic message delivered by all previous prophets. Serving as the ultimate criterion (*al-Furqān*), the Quran corrects human textual corruptions and theological interpolations introduced into earlier scriptures. Demonstrating prophetic authenticity, an unlettered Prophet (*al-Nabiyy al-Umiyy*) revealing complex sacred history proves direct celestial inspiration. Unifying human sacred history, Islam presents itself not as a new religion, but as the final, perfected restoration of the primordial faith of Abraham. Empowering interfaith dialogue, the Quran invites Jews and Christians to recognize the fulfillment of their own scriptures in Prophet Muḥammad. Preserving foundational truth, the Quran protects essential monotheistic doctrines from historical loss or distortion. Building unshakeable conviction, studying the deep coherence of divine revelation across history strengthens believer faith.

**The Eternal Nature of the Intellectual Miracle (Al-Muʿjizah Al-ʿAqliyyah)**

Unlike physical miracles granted to earlier prophets—such as Mūsā's staff turning into a serpent or ʿĪsā healing the blind—which were limited in time and witnessed only by immediate onlookers, the miracle granted to Prophet Muḥammad is an eternal, intellectual miracle: the Holy Quran. Anyone across any century or continent can read, analyze, test, and experience the living miracle of the Quran directly. Defining the intellectual miracle (*al-muʿjizah al-ʿaqliyyah*), the Quran appeals directly to human intellect, reason, and heart reflection. Ensuring universal accessibility across centuries, the Quran remains physically preserved, linguistically pristine, and endlessly relevant for all generations. Challenging humanity across time (*al-Taḥaddī*), the Quran explicitly challenges all humans and jinn to produce a single surah like it (2:23). Transforming human consciousness, the Quranic miracle works from within, purifying character, establishing justice, and elevating intellect. Outlasting physical spectacles, physical miracles become historical reports, while the Quran remains a living, tangible experience today. Inspiring academic and spiritual exploration, every era unlocks deeper linguistic, scientific, and civilizational wisdom from Quranic text. Anchoring global faith, the unalterable Quran unites over a billion believers around identical divine words.

**Universal Framework for Recognizing Clear Divine Evidence**

Verse 133 provides a universal invitation to every rational soul. Abandon stubborn demands for arbitrary spectacles and bad-faith skepticism. Examine the clear, living proof already present in your hands—the Holy Quran (*bayyinatu mā fī al-ṣuḥufi al-ūlā*). Reflect upon its unbroken harmony with historical revelation, embrace its timeless intellectual miracle, and allow its divine light to guide your heart to eternal salvation.

## Sūrah Ṭā-Hā 20:134

> Had We destroyed them with a torment before this ˹Prophet came˺, they would have surely argued, “Our Lord! If only You had sent us a messenger, we would have followed Your revelations before being humiliated and put to shame.”

God establishes the absolute judicial necessity of sending messengers before enforcing judgment: *wa-law annā ahlaknāhum bi-ʿadhābin min qablihi la-qālū rabbanā law-lā arsalta ilaynā rasūlan fa-nattabiʿa āyātika min qabli an nadhilla wa-nakhzā* ('If We had destroyed them with a punishment before this, they would have said: Our Lord! If only You had sent us a messenger, we would have followed Your signs before we were humbled and disgraced'). Eliminating human legal excuses (*la-qālū rabbanā law-lā...*), sending messengers closes every loophole, leaving humanity fully accountable for their choices. Fulfilling the universal Quranic legal principle: 'We never punish until We have sent a messenger' (*wa-mā kunnā muʿadhdhibīna ḥattā nabʿatha rasūlā*, 17:15).

**Exposing the Hypocrisy of Post-Facto Human Excuses**

Verse 134 exposes a psychological pattern in human behavior. Before receiving guidance, people boastful of their morality claim: 'If only God sent us a guide, we would be the most obedient believers!' Yet when a prophet arrives with explicit scriptures (*āyāt*), many of those same people reject him out of arrogance, social pride, and desire worship. Their claim that they would have followed guidance is exposed as a hypothetical lie. Exposing hypothetical human excuses, people often claim they would obey guidance under different circumstances while ignoring guidance today. Fulfilling parallel Quranic observations: 'If a warner came to them, they would be more guided than any other nation; but when a warner came, it only increased them in aversion' (35:42). Demonstrating human self-deception, sinners blame lack of evidence rather than confronting their own internal moral rebellion. Closing all legal loopholes on Judgment Day, God presents full documented proof that clear guidance was delivered to human consciousness. Demanding honest self-reflection, believers evaluate their current responsiveness to Quranic commands rather than making empty future promises. Guarding against procrastination, embracing truth immediately upon receiving it prevents hard-heartedness. Sustaining personal accountability, no human being can blame God, society, or parents for their own deliberate choices.

**The Prophetic Mission as the Ultimate Demonstration of Divine Compassion**

The sending of Prophet Muḥammad and the revelation of the Quran represent the highest expression of divine mercy (*Raḥmatan li-l-ʿĀlamīn*, 21:107). God did not leave humanity to wander blindly in moral confusion or suffer sudden destruction. By sending a human messenger who walked among people, spoke their language, modeled sacred law, and delivered clear scriptures, God provided complete, accessible, living guidance. Defining prophetic sending as supreme mercy, messengers provide living, practical roadmaps for human flourishing. Bridging celestial truth and human life, a human prophet models how to implement divine commands in daily marriage, business, and governance. Eliminating ambiguity in moral law, revelation clarifies exact boundaries about justice, worship, family ethics, and human rights. Demonstrating divine patience, God repeatedly sends prophets and revivers (*mujaddidīn*) to awaken heedless societies. Exalting the finality of Prophet Muḥammad, his universal message preserves complete guidance for all humanity until Judgment Day. Inspiring deep gratitude, believers recognize that receiving Quranic guidance is the greatest blessing granted to their lives. Fulfilling the duty of daʿwah, Muslims must convey Quranic guidance compassionately to humanity, leaving no one without warning.

## Sūrah Ṭā-Hā 20:135

> Say ˹to them, O Prophet˺, “Each ˹of us˺ is waiting, so keep waiting! You will soon know who is on the Straight Path and is ˹rightly˺ guided.”

**The Final Prophetic Proclamation and Eschatological Waiting**

Sūrah Ṭā-Hā concludes with a majestic, dignified prophetic proclamation to all deniers and humanity: *qul kullun mutarabbbiṣun fa-tarabbaṣū fa-sa-taʿlamūna man aṣḥābu al-ṣirāṭi al-sawiyyi wa-mani ihtadā* ('Say O Prophet: Each of us is waiting, so wait! You will come to know who are the followers of the even path and who are guided'). God commands Prophet Muḥammad to address his opponents with calm, unshakeable confidence. Both the believers and the deniers are waiting (*kullun mutarabbbiṣ*) for time to unfold and divine decrees to manifest. The deniers wait for the Prophet's movement to fail, while the Prophet waits for divine victory in this world and ultimate vindication in the Hereafter. Soon, all humanity will know with absolute certainty who walked the straight, balanced path (*al-ṣirāṭ al-sawiyy*) and achieved authentic guidance (*mani ihtadā*). Proclaiming prophetic confidence (*qul kullun mutarabbbiṣ*), verse 135 terminates debate with serene, dignified trust in divine outcome. Acknowledging cosmic waiting (*fa-tarabbaṣū*), both truth and falsehood await the inescapable manifestation of divine judgment. Asserting absolute ultimate clarity (*fa-sa-taʿlamūn*), Resurrection Day and historical resolution will expose truth with undeniable certainty. Defining the straight path as *al-ṣirāṭ al-sawiyy* ('the even / balanced path'), authentic monotheism provides perfect moral and intellectual balance. Identifying true guidance (*mani ihtadā*), ultimate success belongs exclusively to those who accepted divine revelation and lived righteously. Concluding Sūrah Ṭā-Hā with majestic symmetry, the sūrah that began with comforting the Prophet (20:2-3) ends with fortifying his absolute victory. Guiding all believers across time, verse 135 imparts serene emotional resilience when facing ideological hostility and worldly opposition.

**The Ultimate Resolution on Judgment Day (Fa-Sa-Taʿlamūn)**

The divine declaration *fa-sa-taʿlamūn* ('you will come to know!') carries a solemn, unyielding weight. In earthly life, deniers indulge in philosophical debates, arrogant mocking, and media propaganda, creating an illusion that truth is subjective or unknowable. Judgment Day shatters all noise, revealing Reality with absolute, undeniable clarity. When the veil is lifted, every human being will know instantly who was guided and who was ruined. Asserting ultimate cognitive clarity (*fa-sa-taʿlamūn*), Resurrection Day terminates all ideological debates and worldly propaganda. Lifting the earthly veil (*kashafnā ʿanka ghiṭā'aka*, 50:22), every soul perceives cosmic reality with absolute, undeniable sharpness. Vindicating the prophets and believers, those who were mocked on earth stand honored, radiant, and victorious before God. Confirming the tragedy of deniers, those who boasted of worldly wisdom realize too late that they walked in total blindness. Transforming modern skepticism, awareness of *fa-sa-taʿlamūn* drives wise individuals to seek truth before death closes the trial. Directing human priorities, ultimate knowledge matters when it can still alter moral outcomes—which is during earthly physical life. Anchoring believer tranquility, peace comes from knowing that divine justice will deliver the final, flawless verdict.

**The Perfect Structural Closure of Sūrah Ṭā-Hā**

Sūrah Ṭā-Hā achieves breathtaking literary and theological symmetry in verse 135. The sūrah began in verse 2 addressing Prophet Muḥammad: 'We have not sent down the Quran to you to cause you distress, but only as a Reminder for those who fear God' (20:2-3). It concludes in verse 135 by reassuring the Prophet that those who reject the Reminder may wait, but ultimate victory and guidance belong to those on the balanced path. The entire sūrah flows as an unshakeable message of divine comfort, pastoral fortitude, monotheistic triumph, and eschatological clarity. Achieving breathtaking literary symmetry, verse 135 connects seamlessly with the opening verses of Sūrah Ṭā-Hā. Reaffirming the primary purpose of revelation, the Quran is sent not to cause distress (*mā anzalnā ʿalayka al-qur'āna li-tashqā*), but to elevate the soul into peace. Summarizing core surah themes, the narrative of Mūsā, the warnings against the golden calf, the story of Adam, and eschatological scenes all converge on *al-ṣirāṭ al-sawiyy*. Fortifying prophetic leadership, Sūrah Ṭā-Hā equips advocates of truth to stand firm against Pharaohs and Sāmiris of every era. Inaugurating lifelong spiritual devotion, the surah leaves the reader with deep reverential awe, love for prayer, and longing for divine proximity. Praising divine authorship, the flawless structural coherence of Sūrah Ṭā-Hā testifies to the miraculous nature of the Holy Quran. Inspiring continuous recitation, meditating upon Sūrah Ṭā-Hā fills the believer's heart with light, courage, and unshakeable faith.
